&=\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}\Big|\int\limits_{\R^{d}}\phi(z)\big\{U(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U(x,y)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}z\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\int\limits_{B_{1}}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}\big|U(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\end{split}

Lemma 3.63
.

Assume
\Omega\subset\R^{d}
is an open set. Let
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
be open and such that
\operatorname{dist}(\Omega^{\prime},\partial\Omega)>0
(if
\Omega\neq\R^{d}
). For
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
then
\phi_{\delta}*u\in C^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})\cap W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime})
for all
0<\delta<\dist(\Omega^{\prime},\partial\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta}*u-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

The same is true with
W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime})
replaced with
W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime}|\R^{d})
. Moreover if
\Omega=\R^{d}
then one can take
\Omega^{\prime}=\R^{d}
.

Proof.
.

We will only prove the first statement and the others statements will follow analogously. Fix
0<\delta<\dist(\Omega^{\prime},\partial\Omega)
. Note that for
x,y\in\Omega^{\prime}
and
z\in B_{1}
, then
x-\delta z,y-\delta z\in\Omega^{\prime}+B_{\delta}\subset\Omega
. Hence, by mimicking the estimate (
3.19
) and using a trivial change of variables we find that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\phi_{\delta}*u|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime})}&\leq\int\limits_{B_{1}}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\Omega^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime}}\big|U(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq\int\limits_{B_{1}}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\Omega^{\prime}+B_{\delta}\,\Omega^{\prime}+B_{\delta}}\big|U(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq|u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}<\infty.\end{split}

Thus,
\phi_{\delta}*u\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime})
. By continuity of the shift in
L^{p}(\R^{d}\times\R^{d}),

\iint\limits_{\Omega^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime}}\Big|U(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U(x,y)\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

Further, we have

\phi(z)\iint\limits_{\Omega^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime}}\Big|U(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U(x,y)\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2^{p}\phi(z)\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}\big|U(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2^{p}\phi(z)\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\in L^{1}(\R^{d})

By dominated convergence we conclude that

|\phi_{\delta}*u-u|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0
and according to Theorem
3.3
we know that
\|\phi_{\delta}*u-u\|_{L^{p}(\Omega^{\prime})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0
and so we get
\|\phi_{\delta}*u-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega^{\prime}|\R^{d})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

∎

The next lemma plays a determinant role in approximation functions.

Lemma 3.64
.

Assume
\Omega\subset\R^{d}
is open. The following assertions are true

1.

Let
\Omega^{\prime}\subset\Omega
be open and such that
\operatorname{dist}(\Omega^{\prime},\partial\Omega)>0
. For
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
with
\operatorname{supp}u\subset\Omega^{\prime}
the zero extension
\overline{u}
of
u
outside
\Omega
belongs to
W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
. Moreover, there is
C>0
independent of
u
with

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq C\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}.

2.

Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
then for all
u\in L^{p}(\Omega)
and all
x,y\in\R^{d},
we have the estimate

(3.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\varphi(x)u(x)-\varphi(y)u(y)|^{p}&\leq 2^{p}\|\varphi\|^{p}_{W^{1,\infty}}\Big(\mathds{1}_{\operatorname{supp}\varphi}(y)|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}+|u(x)|^{p}(1\land|x-y|^{p})\Big).\end{split}

3.

Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
. If
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
then
\varphi u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
and for some constant independent of
u
,

\displaystyle\|\varphi u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}.

The same holds with
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
replaced by
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

4.

Let
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
. If
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
then
\varphi u\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
and for some constant independent of
u

\displaystyle\|\varphi u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq C\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}.

The same holds true with
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
replaced by
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.
.

Observe that
(iv)
follows by combining
(i)
and
(iii)
. Next, let us prove
(i)
. Set
\delta=\operatorname{dist}(\Omega^{\prime},\partial\Omega)>0
and
C_{\delta}=\int_{|h|\geq\delta}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h>0
. It follows that

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\R^{d}\R^{d}}|\overline{u}(x)-\overline{u}(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x+2\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\R^{d}\setminus\Omega}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq|u|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}+2\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{|x-y|\geq\delta}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=|u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}+2C_{\delta}\|u\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}.

Whence,
\|\overline{u}\|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq(2C_{\delta}+1)\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}
. Now we prove
(ii)
.
Observe that for all
x,y\in\R^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\varphi(x)u(x)-\varphi(y)u(y)|&\leq|\varphi(y)||u(x)-u(y)|+|u(x)||\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|\\
&=|\varphi(y)|\mathds{1}_{\operatorname{supp}\varphi}(y)|u(x)-u(y)|+|u(x)|\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\nabla\varphi(x+t(y-x))\cdot(y-x)\Big|\\
&\leq\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\R^{d})}\mathds{1}_{\operatorname{supp}\varphi}(y)|u(x)-u(y)|+\|\varphi\|_{W^{1,\infty}}|u(x)|(1\land|x-y|).\end{split}

Thus implies
(ii)
follows.
On the other hand,
(iii)
follows by integrating both sides of the estimate (
3.20
), over
\R^{d}\times\R^{d}
, with respect to the measure
\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x
. Indeed, letting
\delta=\dist(\supp\varphi,\Omega^{c})>0
we have

(3.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iint\limits_{\R^{d}\R^{d}}&|\varphi(x)u(x)-\varphi(y)u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\\
&\leq 2^{p}\|\varphi\|^{p}_{W^{1,\infty}}\Big(\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y+\int\limits_{\Omega}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int\limits_{\R^{d}}(1\land|h|^{p})\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\Big)\\
&+2\int\limits_{\operatorname{supp}\varphi}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int\limits_{|h|\geq\delta}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h.\end{split}

∎

Lemma 3.65
.

Assume that
\Omega\subset\R^{d}
is open. Let
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
such that
0\leq\chi\leq 1
and
\chi(x)=1
for
|x|<1
and
\chi(x)=0
for
|x|\geq 2
. If
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
then
\chi(\cdot/j)u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
and

\|\chi(\cdot/j)u-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{j\to\infty}0.

The same holds with
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
replaced by
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Proof.
.

Pointwise we have
\chi(x/j)\xrightarrow{j\to\infty}1
. So that for
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
,
\chi(x/j)u(x)\xrightarrow{j\to\infty}u(x)
. We know that
\chi(\cdot/j)u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. On the other hand,
|\chi(\cdot/j)u|\leq|u|
then
|\chi(\cdot/j)u-u|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0
and using the estimate (
3.20
), for all
x,y\in\R^{d}
and
j\geq 1
we find that

\displaystyle|\chi(x/j)u(x)-\chi(y/j)u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)

\displaystyle\leq|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)

\displaystyle+2^{p}\|\chi\|^{p}_{W^{1,\infty}}|u(x)|^{p}(1\land|x-y|^{p})\nu(x-y)\in L^{1}(\Omega\times\Omega).

The dominated convergence theorem implies that
|\chi(\cdot/j)u-u|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{j\to\infty}0
and thus, it follows that
\|\chi(\cdot/j)u-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{j\to\infty}0.
The case
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
follows likewise.
∎

Let us immediately start with a simple case.

Theorem 3.66
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
\nu:\R^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
satisfying (

J
1

). Then
C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
is dense in
W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
, i.e. for a function
u\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
, there exist functions
u_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
such that

\|u_{n}-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\xrightarrow{n\to\infty}0.

Proof.
.

Let
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
such that
0\leq\chi\leq 1
and
\chi(x)=1
for
|x|<1
and
\chi(x)=0
for
|x|\geq 2
. Letting
\overline{u}_{j}=\chi(\cdot/j)u
, by Lemma
3.65
we can find
j\geq 1
such that
\|\overline{u}_{j}-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}<\varepsilon/2
with
\varepsilon>0
. Note that
\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
. Setting
U_{j}(x,y)=(\overline{u}_{j}(x)-\overline{u}_{j}(y))\nu^{1/p}(x-y)\in L^{p}(\R^{d}\times\R^{d}).
By mimicking the estimate (
3.19
) for
\Omega=\R^{d}
we have

\displaystyle|\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}-\overline{u}_{j}|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq\int\limits_{B_{1}}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\R^{d}\R^{d}}\big|U_{j}(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U_{j}(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

By the continuity of the shift in
L^{p}(\R^{d}\times\R^{d})

\iint\limits_{\R^{d}\,\R^{d}}\Big|U_{j}(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U_{j}(x,y)\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

Further, we have

\phi(z)\iint\limits_{\R^{d}\,\R^{d}}\Big|U_{j}(x-\delta z,y-\delta z)-U_{j}(x,y)\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq 2^{p}\phi(z)\iint\limits_{\R^{d}\,\R^{d}}\big|U_{j}(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x=2^{p}\phi(z)|u|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\in L^{1}(\R^{d}).

By dominated convergence we conclude that
|\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}-\overline{u}_{j}|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0
and according to Theorem
3.3
we know that
\|\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}-\overline{u}_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\R^{d})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0
. Accordingly we can find
\delta>0
sufficiently small such that
\|\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}-\overline{u}_{j}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}<\varepsilon/2
.
Finally we get
\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})
and it is not difficult to show that
\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
and we have

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq\|\overline{u}_{j}-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}+\|\phi_{\delta}*\overline{u}_{j}-\overline{u}_{j}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}<\varepsilon.

∎

We now deal with what can be seen as the Meyers-Serrin density type result for nonlocal spaces.

Theorem 3.67
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
\nu:\R^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
satisfying (

J
1

). Then
C^{\infty}(\Omega)\cap W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
is dense in
\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
.

Proof.
.

We shall only assume
\Omega\neq\R^{d}
because the case
\Omega=\R^{d}
is greatly covered by Theorem
3.66
. We follow the standard arguments as for the classical Sobolev spaces.
Set

O_{j}=\big\{x\in\Omega:\dist(x,\partial\Omega)>2^{-j},\,\,|x|<2^{j}\big\}

and
V_{1}=O_{3}
and
V_{j}=O_{j+2}\setminus\overline{O}_{j}
for
j\geq 2
. We have
\Omega=\bigcup\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}V_{j}
. Consider
(\xi_{j})_{j}
, a partition of the unity subordinate to the family
(V_{j})_{j}
. That is,

\displaystyle\xi_{j}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d}),\quad\supp(\xi_{j})\subset V_{j},\quad 0\leq\xi_{j}\leq 1,\quad\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\xi_{j}=1.

According to Lemma
3.64
for
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
, each
u_{j}=\xi_{j}u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. We also have
\supp u_{j}\subset V_{j}\subset\Omega_{j+2}
.
Chose
\delta>0
small enough so that
\supp u_{j}\cup\supp(\varphi*u_{j})\subset O_{j+3}\setminus O_{j-1}
. Note that
\dist(O_{j+3},\partial\Omega)>2^{-j-3}
. In accordance to Lemma
3.63
we have

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}-u_{j}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(O_{j+4})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

On the other hand, since
\dist(O_{j+3},\Omega\setminus O_{j+4})\geq 2^{-1}
, letting
C=\int_{|h|\geq 2^{-1}}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h>0
we get

\displaystyle\int_{O_{j+3}}|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}(x)-u_{j}(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\Omega\setminus O_{j+4}}\hskip-17.22217pt\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq C\int_{O_{j+3}}|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}(x)-u_{j}(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0.

Altogether we have
\|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}-u_{j}\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0
because

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}-u_{j}\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}=\|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}-u_{j}\|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(O_{j+4})}+2\int_{O_{j+3}}|\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}(x)-u_{j}(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\Omega\setminus O_{j+4}}\hskip-17.22217pt\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y.

Accordingly, given
\varepsilon>0
we can find
\delta_{j}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta_{j}}*u_{j}-u_{j}\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\varepsilon 2^{-j}.

Given that
O_{j+3}\setminus O_{j-1}{}^{{}^{\prime}s}
can only overlap at most five times and
\phi_{\delta}*u_{j}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(O_{j+3}\setminus O_{j-1})
, the function
v=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\phi_{\delta_{j}}*u_{j}
is well defined and belongs to
C^{\infty}(\R^{d})
. Noticing that
u=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}\xi_{j}u=\sum\limits_{j=1}^{\infty}u_{j}
, from the above we get on the one hand that
\|v-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\varepsilon
since

\displaystyle\|v-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\phi_{\delta_{j}}*u_{j}-u_{j}\Big\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|\phi_{\delta_{j}}*u_{j}-u_{j}\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon 2^{-j}=\varepsilon.

And on the other hand,
v\in C^{\infty}(\R^{d})\cap W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
since

\displaystyle\|v\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\|v-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}+\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}+\varepsilon.

This completes the proof.
∎

Note that the main point in the above proof is that if
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
then by truncation it is possible to shrink the support of
u
inside
\Omega
using a suitable partition of unity, so that convoluting makes sense afterwards. On the other hand, the question of approximating a function of
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
by smooth functions is more delicate, but can be reduced to the question whether
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
is closed under the shift operator, i.e. for
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
, do we have
u(\cdot-h)=\tau_{h}u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
? This is equivalent to say that for
\delta>0
sufficiently small,
u\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\Omega+B_{\delta}|\R^{d})
when
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Whereas this is not totally obvious because in this situation the function
u
is already defined on the whole
\R^{d}
. Next we give a partial answer to this question when
\Omega
has a compact Lipschitz boundary.

Definition 3.68
.

An open set
\Omega\subset\R^{d}
shall be called a local graph domain if for each
x\in\partial\Omega
there exists a
r>0
and a function
\gamma:\R^{d-1}\to\R
such that

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B_{r}(x)

\displaystyle=\big\{x\in B_{r}(x):\,x_{d}>\gamma(x^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle\partial\Omega\cap B_{r}(x)

\displaystyle=\big\{x\in B_{r}(x):\,x_{d}=\gamma(x^{\prime})\big\}

\displaystyle\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(x)

\displaystyle=\big\{x\in B_{r}(x):\,x_{d}\leq\gamma(x^{\prime})\big\}.

In addition, if
\gamma
is of class
C^{m,\sigma}
then
\Omega
is called to be a
C^{m,\sigma}
-domain. For
m=0,\sigma=1
,
\Omega
is called a Lipschitz domain.

We will need the following result.

Lemma 3.69
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
and assume
\nu:\R^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
satisfying (

J
1

) is radially almost decreasing, i.e.
\nu(x)\leq c\nu(y)
(
c>0
) whenever
|y|\geq|x|
. Then for
\Omega\subset\R^{d}
open,
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\subset L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\R^{d}).
Moreover for each compact
K\subset\R^{d}
there is a constant
C=(K,\Omega,\nu)
such that for all
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
,

\displaystyle\int_{K}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}.

Proof.
.

If
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
then
u\in L^{p}(\Omega)
. For a compact set
K\subset\R^{d}
write
K=K_{1}\cup K_{2}
with
K_{1}=\Omega\cap K
and
K_{2}=\Omega^{c}\cap K
. Now choose
K^{\prime}\subset\Omega
to be any compact set and
R>0
sufficiently large such that
K^{\prime}\cup K_{2}\subset B_{R}(0)
.
Clearly
u\in L^{p}(K_{1})
and
u\in L^{p}(K^{\prime})
. It remains to show that
u\in L^{p}(K_{2}).
For every
x\in K^{\prime}
and
y\in K_{2}
we have
|x-y|\leq R
so that
\nu(x-y)\geq c_{R}
for some constant
c_{R}>0
. Applying this and Jensen’s inequality we get the following

\displaystyle\infty>\iint\limits_{\Omega\R^{d}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq c_{R}\int_{K^{\prime}}\int_{K_{2}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq c_{R}|K^{\prime}|\int_{K_{2}}|u(x)-\mbox{$\fint_{K^{\prime}}$}u|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x.

The conclusion is reached because

\displaystyle\int_{K_{2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}|K_{2}|\fint_{K^{\prime}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x+2^{p}\int_{K_{2}}|u(x)-\mbox{$\fint_{K^{\prime}}$}u|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}(c_{R}^{-1}+1)|K_{2}||K^{\prime}|^{-1}\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}<\infty.

∎

The following density result ameliorates the analogous one from
[
Voi17
]
whose proof is more likely not to be fully satisfactory.

Theorem 3.70
.

Assume
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is open with a compact Lipschitz boundary
\partial\Omega
. Let
\nu
satisfy (

J
1

) and in addition, assume
\nu
is radially almost decreasing. Then
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
with respect to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, i.e. for
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
there exists a sequence
(u_{n})\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

\displaystyle\|u_{n}-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }n\to\infty\,.

Moreover for
p=2
, then
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
V^{1}_{\nu}(\Omega|\R^{d})
with respect to the norm

\|u\|^{2}_{V^{1}_{\nu}(\Omega|\R^{d})}=\|Lu\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|u\|^{2}_{V_{\nu}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.
.

Let
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We prove that
|v_{\varepsilon}-u|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\to 0}0
where
v_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\R^{d})
. This implies

\|v_{\varepsilon}-u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\to 0}0

because convergence of the
L^{p}
-norms follows by standard arguments. Note that the sequence
(v_{\varepsilon})
is constructed by translation and convolution of the function
u
with a mollifier
Thus for
u\in V^{1}_{\nu}(\Omega|\R^{d})
since
\partial\Omega
is Lipschitz, taking into account Proposition
2.13
, by a subsequence shift arguments one can show that
\|Lu-Lv_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\to 0}0
.

Step 1:
Let
x_{0}\in\partial\Omega
. Since is
\partial\Omega
Lipschitz, there exists
r>0
and a Lipschitz function
\gamma:\R^{d-1}\to\R
with Lipschitz constant
k>0
, such that (upon relabelling the coordinates)

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\{x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})\in B_{r}(x_{0})|x_{d}>\gamma(x^{\prime})\}.

For the sake of convenience, we choose
r>0
so small such that
|\Omega\cap B^{c}_{r}(x_{0})|>0
. For
x\in B_{r/2}(x_{0})
,
\tau>1+k
and
0<\varepsilon<\frac{r}{2(1+\tau)}
we define the shifted point
x_{\varepsilon}=x+\tau\varepsilon e_{d}\,.
We define

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon}(x)=u(x_{\varepsilon})=u(x+\tau\varepsilon e_{d})\quad\text{and}\qquad v_{\varepsilon}=\phi_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{\varepsilon},

where
\phi_{\varepsilon}
is a smooth mollifier having support in
B_{\varepsilon}(0)
. It is noteworthy to emphasize that the smoothness of the function
\phi_{\varepsilon}*u_{\varepsilon}
is warranted by Lemma
3.69
, which assures that
u\in L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\R^{d})
when
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\nu
is radially almost decreasing. Indeed we have
C_{c}^{\infty}(\R^{d})*L^{1}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\R^{d})\subset C^{\infty}(\R^{d})
.

Step 2:
Let us assume

\supp u\Subset B_{r/4}(x_{0})
. In this case
v_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(B_{r}(x_{0}))
. In this step, we aim to prove

|v_{\varepsilon}-u|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{ as }\varepsilon\to 0\,.

Due to the nonlocal nature of the semi-norm, this step turns out to be rather challenging. We begin with a geometric observation.

Lemma 3.71
.

Let
z\in B_{1}(0)
. Let
\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}=\Omega+\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z)
. Then
\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})\subset\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{0})
.

Proof.
.

For
h\in\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})
, let us write
h=t+\varepsilon\tau e_{d}-\varepsilon z
with
t\in\Omega
. Note that since
\varepsilon<\frac{r}{2(\tau+1)}
we get
|t-x_{0}|\leq|t-h|+|h-x_{0}|<\varepsilon(\tau+1)+r/2<r
. So that
t\in B_{r}(x_{0})
,
h^{\prime}=t^{\prime}-\varepsilon z^{\prime}
and
h_{d}=t_{d}+\varepsilon(\tau-z_{d})
. Since
\gamma
is Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant
k<\tau-1
and
t\in\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{0})=\{x\in B_{r}(x_{0})|x_{d}>\gamma(x^{\prime})\}
, i.e.
t_{d}>\gamma(t^{\prime})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma(h^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\gamma(t^{\prime})+|\gamma(h^{\prime})-\gamma(t^{\prime})|<t_{d}+\varepsilon k|z^{\prime}|

\displaystyle<t_{d}+\varepsilon k<t_{d}+\varepsilon(\tau-z_{d})=h_{d}.

Thus,
h\in B_{r}(x_{0})
and
h_{d}>\gamma(h^{\prime})
. We have shown
h\in\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{0})
as desired.
∎

The main technical tool of the argument below is the Vitali convergence theorem, (see Theorem
6.19
in Appendix). Since
u
belongs to the space
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
, for every
\delta>0
there is
\eta>0
such that for all sets
E\subset\Omega
,
F\subset\R^{d}
with
|E\times F|<\eta
we know

(3.22)

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{EF}\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle<\delta\text{ and }\int_{EF}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y<\delta\,.

The second estimate uses the fact that
u
has compact support and belongs to
L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\R^{d})
.

Lemma 3.72
.

For every
\delta>0
there is
\eta>0
such that for all sets
E\subset\Omega
,
F\subset\R^{d}
with
|E\times F|<\eta

(3.23)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{z\in B_{1}(0)}\sup\limits_{\varepsilon>0}\iint\limits_{EF}\big|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x<\delta\,,

where
u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(\xi)=u_{\varepsilon}(\xi-\varepsilon z)=u(\xi+\varepsilon\tau e_{d}-\varepsilon z)
.

Proof.
.

Let
\delta>0
. Choose
\eta>0
as in (
3.22
). Let
\varepsilon>0
,
z\in B_{1}(0)
. Let
E\subset\Omega
,
F\subset\R^{d}
be sets with
|E\times F|<\eta
. Then

(3.24)

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{EF}\big|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x=\iint\limits_{E^{z}_{\varepsilon}F^{z}_{\varepsilon}}\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\,,

where
E^{z}_{\varepsilon}=E+\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z)
and
F^{z}_{\varepsilon}
defined analogously. We decompose
E^{z}_{\varepsilon}
as follows
E^{z}_{\varepsilon}=E^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})\cup E^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})
. Note

E^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})\subset\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})\subset\Omega\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})\,,

where we apply Lemma
3.71
. We directly conclude

(3.25)

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{E^{z}_{\varepsilon}F^{z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r/2}(x_{0})}(x)\big|u(y)-u(x)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\delta\,.

With regard to the remaining term since
\operatorname{supp}u\subset B_{r/4}(x_{0})
, note

(3.26)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\iint\limits_{E^{z}_{\varepsilon}F^{z}_{\varepsilon}}&\mathbbm{1}_{B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})}(x)\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=\iint\limits_{E^{z}_{\varepsilon}F^{z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbbm{1}_{B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}(y)|u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq c(r,\nu)\iint\limits_{E^{z}_{\varepsilon}F^{z}_{\varepsilon}}\mathbbm{1}_{B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}(y)|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq c\iint\limits_{E^{z}_{\varepsilon}F^{z}_{\varepsilon}}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&=c\iint\limits_{EF^{z}_{\varepsilon}}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\leq c\delta\,.\end{split}

The positive constant
c(r,\nu)
depends on
r
and on the shape of
\nu
. Summation over (
3.25
) and (
3.26
) completes the proof after redefining
\delta
accordingly.
∎

The next lemma shows the tightness of
u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)
uniformly for
z\in B_{1}(0)
and
\varepsilon>0
.

Lemma 3.73
.

For every
\delta>0
there exists
E(\delta)\subset\Omega
and
F_{\delta}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
|E(\delta)\times F_{\delta}|<\infty
and

(3.27)

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{z\in B_{1}(0)}\sup\limits_{\varepsilon>0}\iint\limits_{(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\setminus(E(\delta)\times F_{\delta})}\big|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x<\delta.

Proof.
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
and
z\in B_{1}(0)
. Let
\bar{R}=\sup\limits_{\xi\in\Omega}|\xi-x_{0}|
which is finite since
\Omega
is bounded. Note that
\supp u^{z}_{\varepsilon}\subset B_{r/2}(x_{0})
. Choose
R>0
so large such that
[B^{c}_{R}(x_{0})]_{\varepsilon}^{z}=B^{c}_{R}(x_{0})+\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}+z)\subset B^{c}_{R/2}(x_{0})
and
|x-y|\geq R/2-\bar{R}
for
x\in B^{c}_{R/2}(x_{0})
and
y\in\Omega
. Thus,

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{(\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d})\setminus(\Omega\times B_{R}(x_{0}))}

\displaystyle\big|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x=\iint\limits_{\Omega B^{c}_{R}(x_{0})}\big|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int\limits_{[B^{c}_{R}(x_{0})]_{\varepsilon}^{z}}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\int\limits_{\Omega}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int\limits_{B^{c}_{R/2-\bar{R}}(x)}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\|u\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\int\limits_{B^{c}_{R/2-\bar{R}}(0)}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h.

The desired result follows by taking
E(\delta)=\Omega
and
F_{\delta}=B_{R}(x_{0})
with
R>0
large enough such that
\int\limits_{B^{c}_{R/2-\bar{R}}(0)}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h<\delta\|u\|^{-2}_{L^{p}(\Omega)}
.
∎

Lemma 3.74
.

There exists a constant
C(\Omega,r,\nu)
depending on
\Omega,r
and
\nu
such that

(3.28)

\displaystyle|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C(\Omega,r,\nu)|u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\quad\text{for all $z\in B_{1}(0)$ and all $\varepsilon>0$}.\quad

Proof.
.

Note that
|x-y|\geq r/4
for
x\in B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})
and
y\in B_{r/4}(x_{0})
. Note that is
\nu
radially almost decreasing, i.e.
\nu(x-y)<c(r,\nu)=c\nu(r/4)
. Let us choose

C=1+\hskip-12.91663pt\sup_{y\in B_{r/4}(x_{0})}\Big(\hskip-4.30554pt\int\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{r}^{c}(x_{0})}\hskip-8.61108pt\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{-1}\hskip-8.61108pt\int\limits_{B^{c}_{r/4}(0)}\hskip-8.61108pt\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\leq 1+(c(r,\nu)|\Omega\cap B_{r}^{c}(x_{0})|)^{-1}\hskip-12.91663pt\int\limits_{B^{c}_{r/4}(0)}\hskip-8.61108pt\nu(h)<\infty.

Therefore, for each
z\in B_{1}(0)
and each
\varepsilon>0
we have

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}\left|u(x)|^{p}-u(y)\right|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\int\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\int\limits_{B^{c}_{r/4}(y)}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip-12.91663pt\int\limits_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\int\limits_{\Omega\cap B^{c}_{r}(x_{0})}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=C\hskip-12.91663pt\iint\limits_{\Omega\cap B^{c}_{r}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x.\

By applying change of variables, this and Lemma
3.71
, we have

\displaystyle|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\iint\limits_{\Omega\R^{d}}\big|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\iint\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\R^{d}}\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\iint\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B_{r/2}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}\hskip-8.61108pt\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\hskip-12.91663pt\iint\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\hskip-12.91663pt\iint\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}\hskip-8.61108pt\big|u(x)-u(y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+C\hskip-12.91663pt\iint\limits_{\Omega\cap B^{c}_{r}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C|u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

∎

We are now in a position to prove the main result of this step. By mimicking the estimate (
3.19
), we get

\displaystyle|v_{\varepsilon}-u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\iint\limits_{\Omega\,\R^{d}}((v_{\varepsilon}(x)-v_{\varepsilon}(y))-(u(x)-u(y))|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{B_{1}(0)}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\Omega\R^{d}}\big|(u_{\varepsilon}(x-\varepsilon z)-u_{\varepsilon}(y-\varepsilon z))-(u(x)-u(y))\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\,\,

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{B_{1}(0)}|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}-u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\,.

For each fixed
z\in B_{1}(0)
the family of functions

(x,y)\mapsto\big|(u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)-u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(y))-(u(x)-u(y))\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)
with
(x,y)\in\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\varepsilon>0
is equiintegrable (by Lemma
3.72
), is tight (by Lemma
3.73
) and converges to
0
a.e on
\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Also note that according to the estimate (
3.28
), each member of this family is integrable (this follows from the equiintegrability). Thus for fixed
z\in B_{1}(0)
the Vitali’s convergence theorem gives

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega\,\R^{d}}\big|(u_{\varepsilon}(x-\varepsilon z)-u_{\varepsilon}(y-\varepsilon z))-(u(x)-u(y))\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\ \,\mathrm{d}y\ \,\mathrm{d}x\overset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\longrightarrow}0\,.

That is,
|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}-u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\to 0,
as
\varepsilon\to 0
for each
z\in B_{1}(0)
. Further, from estimate (
3.28
) the function
z\mapsto\phi(z)[u^{z}_{\varepsilon}-u]^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}
is bounded by
2C[u]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}
for all
\varepsilon>0
and a.e.
z\in B_{1}(0)
. Thus, by Lebesgue’s
dominated convergence theorem

\int\limits_{B_{1}(0)}|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}-u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\overset{\varepsilon\to 0}{\longrightarrow 0}.

This implies
[v_{\varepsilon}-u]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Step 3:
Let
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
be arbitrary. Let
R>0
such that
\Omega\subset B_{R}(0)
. Let
f_{R}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{3R}(0))
with
f_{R}\leq 1
and

f_{R}(x)=1
for all
x\in B_{2R}(0)
.
Define
u_{R}=f_{R}u
. Then according to Lemma
3.65
, we have
\supp(u_{R})\subset B_{3R}(0)
,
u_{R}\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
and
[u-u_{R}]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\to 0
as
R\to\infty
.

Step 4:
Let
x_{i}\in\partial\Omega
,
r_{i}>0
,
i=1,..,N
, such that

\partial\Omega\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}B_{r_{i}/2}(x_{i}),

where the
r_{i}
are chosen small enough, such that up to relabelling the coordinates, we can assume

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B_{4r_{i}}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=\{x\in B_{4r_{i}}(x_{i})|x_{d}>\gamma_{i}(x^{\prime})\}

for some smooth
\gamma_{i}:\R^{d-1}\to\R
as in Step 1. Let
\Omega^{*}=\{x\in\R^{d}|\dist(x,\Omega)>\frac{1}{2}\min_{i=\{1,..,N\}}r_{i}\}

and
\Omega_{0}=\{x\in\Omega|\dist(x,\Omega^{c})>\frac{1}{2}\min_{i=\{1,..,N\}}r_{i}\}
. Then

\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\cup\Omega^{*}\cup\Omega_{0}=\R^{d}.

Let
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=0}^{N+1}
be a smooth partition of unity subordinated to the above constructed sets.
We define

u_{i}=\xi_{i}\cdot u_{R}\text{ for all }i\in\{0,..,N+1\},

and thus

\displaystyle\supp u_{i}\subset B_{r_{i}}(x_{i})\text{ for }i\in\{1,..N\},

\displaystyle\supp u_{0}\subset\Omega_{0},

\displaystyle\supp u_{N+1}\subset\Omega^{*}.

Step 5:
Let
\delta>0
and
i\in\{1,..,N\}
. By Step 2 there exists a sequence
v^{i}_{\varepsilon}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{4r_{i}}(x_{i}))
such that

[u_{i}-v^{i}_{\varepsilon}]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\longrightarrow 0

for
\varepsilon\to 0
. Thus we can choose
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
[u_{i}-v^{i}_{\varepsilon}]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\frac{\delta}{N+2}

for all
i\in\{1,..,N\}
.

For
i=N+1
define
v^{N+1}_{\varepsilon}=\phi_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{N+1}
and set
r=\frac{1}{4}\min_{i\in\{1,..,N\}}r_{i}
.
Choosing
\varepsilon<r
and since

\supp u_{N+1}\subset\Omega^{*}
for all
x\in\Omega
,
y\in\R^{d}
and
z\in B_{\varepsilon}(0)

U_{N+1}(x,y)=U_{N+1}(x-z,y-z)=0\quad\text{ or }\quad\left|x-y\right|>r.

where we set
U_{N+1}(x,y)=(u(x)-u(y)\nu^{1/p}(x-y)
.
Thus, following the estimate (
3.19
) , we have

\displaystyle|v^{N+1}_{\varepsilon}-u_{N+1}|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=|\phi_{\varepsilon}*u^{N+1}_{\varepsilon}-u_{N+1}|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{1}(0)}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\Omega\R^{d}}|U_{N+1}(x-\varepsilon z,y-\varepsilon z)-U_{N+1}(x,y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x.

By the continuity of the shift in
L^{p}(\R^{d}\times\R^{d})
,

\iint\limits_{\Omega\,\R^{d}}|U_{N+1}(x-\varepsilon z,y-\varepsilon z)-U_{N+1}(x,y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\longrightarrow 0.

Further, since
U_{N+1}(x,y)=U_{N+1}(x-z,y-z)=0
or
\left|x-y\right|>r,
for any
z\in B_{1}(0)
, then the map

z\mapsto\phi(z)\iint\limits_{\Omega\,\R^{d}}U_{N+1}(x-\varepsilon z,y-\varepsilon z)-U_{N+1}(x,y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

is bounded. Thus
[v^{N+1}_{\varepsilon}-u_{N+1}]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\to 0
by dominated convergence and we find

\varepsilon_{0}>0
, such that
|v^{N+1}_{\varepsilon}-u_{N+1}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\frac{\delta}{N+2}
for all
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
.
We define
v^{0}_{\varepsilon}=\phi_{\varepsilon}\ast u_{0}
. Thus for
\varepsilon<r

\supp v^{0}_{\varepsilon}\Subset\Omega.

The convergence
v^{0}_{\varepsilon}\to u_{0}
follows by the same arguments as above and we find
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such
that
[v_{\varepsilon}^{0}-u_{0}]_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\frac{\delta}{N+2}
for all
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
.

Step 6:
Define
v_{\varepsilon}=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N+1}v^{i}_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\R^{d})
. Since
u_{R}(x)=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N+1}u_{i}(x)
, we have

\displaystyle|u_{R}-v_{\varepsilon}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\sum_{i=0}^{N+1}\big(v^{i}_{\varepsilon}-u_{i}\big)\Big|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{N+1}|v^{i}_{\varepsilon}-u_{i}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq(N+2)\frac{\delta}{N+2}.

Choosing
\frac{1}{\varepsilon}<R
in Step 3 such that
|u-u_{R}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\delta
, concludes

|u-v_{\varepsilon}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq|u-u_{R}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}+|u_{R}-v_{\varepsilon}|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2\delta.

The convergence in
L^{p}(\Omega)
follows from the continuity of the shift in
L^{p}(\R^{d})
.
∎

We now derive another variant of Theorem
3.70
for
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\cap L^{p}(\R^{d})
only assuming (

J
1

). Recall

{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|u\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}=\|u\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\R^{d})}+\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Theorem 3.75
.

Assume
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is open with a compact Lipschitz boundary
\partial\Omega
. Assume that
\nu
only satisfies (

J
1

). Then,
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is dense in
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{p}(\R^{d})
with respect to the norm
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}
, i.e. for
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
, there exists a sequence
(u_{n})\subset C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|u_{n}-u\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }n\to\infty\,.

Proof.
.

The proof of the assertion here solely follows the scheme of the proof of Theorem
3.70
so we only point out the crucial Step 2 where the radially of
\nu
enters into play. The estimate (
3.28
), i.e.

\displaystyle|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C(\Omega,r,\nu)|u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}

remains true with the constant

C=1+\hskip-12.91663pt\sup_{y\in B_{r/4}(x_{0})}\Big(\hskip-4.30554pt\int\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{r}^{c}(x_{0})}\hskip-8.61108pt\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\Big)^{-1}\hskip-8.61108pt\int\limits_{B^{c}_{r/4}(0)}\hskip-8.61108pt\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h<\infty.

We recall,
|x-y|\geq r/4
for
x\in B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})
and
y\in B_{r/4}(x_{0})
.

However since in this case we have
u\in L^{p}(\R^{d})
it is easy to obtain the following analog estimate

(3.29)

\displaystyle|u^{z}_{\varepsilon}|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C(r,\nu)\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})},

with the mere constant
C=1+\int\limits_{B^{c}_{r/4}(0)}\hskip-8.61108pt\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h<\infty.

Indeed, a close look at the proof of the estimate (
3.29
) shows that one gets the estimate (
3.29
) by applying the following

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})\times\R^{d}}\left|u(x)|^{p}-u(y)\right|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{B_{r/4}(x_{0})}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\int\limits_{\Omega^{z}_{\varepsilon}\cap B^{c}_{r/2}(x_{0})}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\int_{\R^{d}}|u(y)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y.

Next by mimicking the estimate (
3.19
) we have

\displaystyle|v_{\varepsilon}-u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\int\limits_{B_{1}}\phi(z)\,\mathrm{d}z\iint\limits_{\Omega\R^{d}}\big|U(x-\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z),y-\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z))-U(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

with
U(x,y)=(u(x)-u(y))\nu^{1/p}(x-y)
. The estimate (
3.29
) shows that
u,u^{z}_{\varepsilon}\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
we recall
u^{z}_{\varepsilon}(x)=u(x+\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z))
equivalently
U(\cdot-\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z),\cdot-\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z)),U\in L^{p}(\Omega\times\R^{d})
. So that by continuity of the shift,

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega\R^{d}}\big|U(x-\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z),y-\varepsilon(\tau e_{d}-z))-U(x,y)\big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\to 0}0.

Analogously by invoking dominated convergence we get
|v_{\varepsilon}-u|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\to 0}0
and step 2 follows. The remaining details from the proof of Theorem
3.70
are unchanged. However we emphasize that the smoothness of
v_{\varepsilon}=\phi_{\varepsilon}*u_{\varepsilon}
is provided since
u\in L^{p}(\R^{d})\subset L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\R^{d})
.
∎

Theorem 3.76
.

Let
\Omega
be a continuous domain such that the boundary
\partial\Omega
is compact. Assume
\nu
satisfies
(

J
1

) (see page

J
1

). The following assertions hold.

1.

C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
is dense in
W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

2.

C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
is dense in
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
, where
C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
is the space of all functions which are restriction of
C^{\infty}
functions with compact support in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\Omega
.

Remark 3.77
.

Note that an incomplete proof of Theorem
3.76

(i)
is provided in
[
BGPR20
, Theorem A.4]
under the additional condition that
\nu
radially almost decreasing. However for our setting, this is just reminiscent of the main result in
[
FSV15
]
for fractional Sobolev spaces from which we borrow some ideas.

Proof.
.

(i)
Since
\partial\Omega
is compact, let
x_{i}\in\partial\Omega
,
i=1,..,N
and
r>0
such that

\partial\Omega\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}B_{r/2}(x_{i}),

where the
r>0
is chosen small enough, such that up to relabelling the coordinates, we can assume

\displaystyle\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=\{x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})\in B_{r}(x_{i})|x_{d}>\gamma_{i}(x^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=\{x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})\in B_{r}(x_{i})|x_{d}\leq\gamma_{i}(x^{\prime})\}.

for some continuous functions
\gamma_{i}:\R^{d-1}\to\R
. Let
\Omega_{r/2}=\{x\in\Omega:\dist(x,\partial\Omega)>r/2\}
. Then,

\Omega\subset\bigcup_{i=1}^{N}B_{r}(x_{i})\cup\Omega_{r}.

Let
\{\xi_{i}\}_{i=0}^{N}
be a smooth partition of unity subordinated to the above constructed sets. That is we have
\xi_{i}\geq 0
,
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}\xi_{i}=1
and
\xi_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega_{r/2})
and
\xi_{i}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{r}(x_{i})).
Let
u\in W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
and define

u_{i}=\xi_{i}\cdot u\quad\text{ for all }i\in\{0,..,N\}.

For
0<\eta<r/2
sufficiently small, we define

\displaystyle u^{i}_{\eta}(x)=u^{i}(x^{\prime},x_{d}-\eta)\quad\text{ for all }i\in\{0,..,N\}.

Recall that
u=0
on
\Omega^{c}
, so it is clear that
u\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
and according to Lemma
3.64

u^{i}=\xi_{i}u\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
. Therefore, regarding the finiteness of the
\|u^{i}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}
, no problem should raise while shifting the function
u^{i}
. Thus
u^{i},u^{i}_{\eta}\in W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})
. Invoking the continuity of the shift in both
L^{p}(\R^{d})
and
L^{p}(\R^{d}\times\R^{d})
, for instance like in the proof of Theorem
3.3
, we find that
\|u^{i}-u^{i}_{\eta}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\xrightarrow{\eta\to 0}0
. Given
\varepsilon>0
, from now we fix
\eta>0
sufficiently small that

\displaystyle\|u^{i}-u^{i}_{\eta}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2(N+1)}.

Meanwhile we claim that
u^{i}_{\eta}
is compactly supported in
\Omega
. We even have
\supp u^{i}_{\eta}\subset\overline{V^{i}_{\eta}}
with
V^{i}_{\eta}=\{x\in B_{r}(x_{i}):\,x_{d}-\eta>\gamma_{i}(x^{\prime})\}.
Indeed assume

x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})\not\in V^{i}_{\eta}=\{z=(z^{\prime},z_{d})\in B_{r}(x_{i}):\,_{d}-\eta>\gamma_{i}(z^{\prime})\}

equivalently

(x^{\prime},x_{d}-\eta)\in\{z=(z^{\prime},z_{d})\in B_{r}(x_{i}):\,z_{d}\leq\gamma_{i}(z^{\prime})\}=\Omega^{c}\cap B_{r}(x_{i}).

Which implies
u^{i}_{\eta}(x)=[\xi_{i}u](x^{\prime},x_{d}-\eta)=0
because
(x^{\prime},x_{d}-\eta)\in\Omega^{c}
and
u^{i}=\xi_{i}u=0
on
\Omega^{c}
. We now get

\supp u^{i}_{\eta}\subset\overline{V^{i}_{\eta}}\subset\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{i}).

Indeed, by the continuity of the
\gamma_{i}^{\prime}s
we get

\overline{V^{i}_{\eta}}=\{x\in B_{r}(x_{i}):\,x_{d}-\eta\geq\gamma_{i}(x^{\prime})\}\subset\{x\in B_{r}(x_{i}):\,x_{d}>\gamma_{i}(x^{\prime})\}=\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{i}).

Meanwhile the same arguments used in the proof of Theorem
3.3
yields that

\|\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}-u^{i}_{\eta}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0
.
For
0<\delta<\dist(\supp u^{i}_{\eta},\partial\Omega)/2
, we have

\displaystyle\supp\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}\subset\overline{B_{\delta}+\supp u^{i}_{\eta}}\subset\Omega.

We have
\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
. We can find
0<\delta<\dist(\supp u^{i}_{\eta},\partial\Omega)/2
such that
\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}-u^{i}_{\eta}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2(N+1)}.

For
u_{0}
the assertion
\phi_{\delta}*u_{0}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, is much easier since there is no need to shift it. Of course
v=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
and
u=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}u^{i}
since
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}\xi_{i}=1
.
Altogether we get

\displaystyle\|v-u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum_{i=0}^{N}\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}-u^{i}\Big\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{N}\big[\|\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}-u^{i}_{\eta}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}+\|u^{i}_{\eta}-u^{i}\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\R^{d})}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{N}\frac{\varepsilon}{N+1}=\varepsilon.

To show
(ii)
, in view of Theorem
3.67
it suffices to prove it for
u\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)\cap W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. With the previous notations we consider
u^{i}_{\eta}(x)=[u\xi_{i}](x^{\prime},x_{d}+\eta)
,

i=1,\cdots,N
. Note that
u^{i}_{\eta}
is
C^{\infty}
and supported in
B_{r}(x_{i})
because
\supp\xi_{i}\subset B_{r}(x_{i})
. Furthermore, for
0<\eta<r
small enough, it is possible to show that
x\in\Omega^{c}\cap B_{\eta/2}(x_{i})
implies
(x^{\prime},x_{d}+\eta)\in\Omega\cap B_{r}(x_{i})
and hence
u^{i}_{\eta}(x^{\prime},x_{d}+\eta)
is well define.
In other words,
u^{i}_{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
. Also note that for
\eta>0
small enough we also
have
u^{i}_{\eta}\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. With analogous arguments as previously, for
v=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N}\phi_{\delta}*u^{i}_{\eta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\overline{\Omega})
one gets
\|v-u\|<\varepsilon
.

∎

Now, we state an extension result for the space
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. Up to a rigorous modification of the extension result of
[
NPV12
, Theorem 5.4]
one
is able to obtain the following extension theorem.

Theorem 3.78
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
and
\Omega
be open with a bounded Lipschitz boundary. Let
\nu:\R^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
be radial, almost decreasing and satisfies the
p
-Lévy integrability (

J
1

). Then there exists linear bounded operator
E:W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\to W_{\nu}^{p}(\R^{d})
such that
Eu|_{\Omega}=u
for all
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
.

If the conclusion of the above theorem holds then one says that
\Omega
is an extension domain for
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. Let us recall, in the special case where
\nu(h)=|h|^{-d-sp}
,
s\in(0,1)
, the main result of
[
Zho15
]
states that
\Omega
is an extension domain for
W^{s,p}(\Omega)
if and only if
\Omega
is a
d
-set, i.e. there exists
c>0
such that for every
x\in\partial\Omega
and
r\in(0,1)
we have
|B(x,r)\cap\Omega|\geq cr^{d}
.

13
Compact embeddings and Poincaré type inequalities

In this section we are concerned with the compact embeddings of the spaces
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
,
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
L^{p}(\Omega)
. Let us start with some basic observations and formulate some sufficient assumptions on
\nu
and
\Omega
. We shall temporarily modify our general assumption on the function
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
. Like previously for
1\leq p<\infty
, we assume that
\nu
satisfies

(
I_{1}
)

\displaystyle\nu(-h)=\nu(h)~\text{ for all $h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$ and}~\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1\land|h|^{p})\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h<\infty\,.

It is an obvious fact that if
\nu\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then the space
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
coincides with
L^{p}(\Omega)
and thus cannot be compactly embedded into
L^{p}(\Omega)
. Likewise, if
\nu\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, then the spaces
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
coincide with
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
which is not even locally compactly embedded in
L^{p}(\Omega)
. In other words, the least necessary condition for compact embeddings to hold is that
\nu
must not be integrable. Therefore, it is necessary to consider the following non-integrability condition

(
I_{2}
)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h=\infty.

If the condition (

I
1

) holds true, it is possible to strengthen the condition (

I
2

) by merely assuming that

(
I^{\prime}_{2}
)

\displaystyle\lim_{|h|\to 0}|h|^{d}\nu(h)=\infty.

The conditions (

I
1

) and (

I
′

2

) clearly imply (

I
1

) and (

I
2

). The latter conditions are sufficient to obtain locally compact embeddings of
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
and
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
into
L^{p}(\Omega)

(see Corollary
3.84
). The global compactness needs some extra regularity assumptions on
\Omega
compatible with
\nu
that we will state later on. First and foremost, we the following well known from functional analysis.

Theorem 3.79

(Chapter X, p.278,
[
Yos80
]
)
.

Given
X
and
Y
two Banach spaces denote by
\mathcal{L}(X,Y)
(resp.
\mathcal{K}(X,Y)
) the space of linear bounded operators( resp. linear compact operators) from
X
into
Y
. Then
\mathcal{K}(X,Y)
is closed in
\mathcal{L}(X,Y))
with respect top the topology associated to the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}(X,Y))}
.

\displaystyle\|T\|_{\mathcal{L}(X,Y))}:=\sup_{\|x\|_{X}=1}\|Tx\|_{Y}.

For a measurable subset
D\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
we adopt the notation
R_{D}
to denote the restriction operator assigning
u|_{D}
to a function
u
. The following lemma is a consequence of Theorem
3.9
applied with
p=r
and
q=1
.

Lemma 3.80
.

Let
w\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then the convolution operator
T_{w}:L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
T_{w}u=w*u
is continuous, locally compact and its norm is not greater than
\|w\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.

In what follows, we denote by
\nu_{\delta}
the kernel
\nu
truncated away from the ball
B_{\delta}(0)
, i.e., for every
h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and every
\delta>0
, we have

\nu_{\delta}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus B_{\delta}(0)}(h)\nu(h).

The following result is reminiscent of
[
JW19a
, Theorem 1.2]
under a slightly weaker assumption. Recall that
u\in W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
if and only if
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u=0
on
\Omega^{c}
.

Theorem 3.81
.

Let
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
be a measurable function for which the conditions (

I
2

) and (

I
′

1

) hold (in particular if (

I
1

) holds true ) with

(
I^{\prime}_{1}
)

\displaystyle\nu(-h)=\nu(h)~\text{ for all $h\in\mathbb{R}^{d}$ and}~\int_{|h|\geq\delta}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h<\infty~\text{ for all $\delta>0$}.

Then the embedding
W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is locally compact. Moreover, for
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
open and bounded, the embedding
W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
is compact.

Proof.
.

This proof is in the spirit of
[
JW19a
]
. Let
\delta>0
small enough, the assumptions (

I
′

1

) and (

I
2

) imply
0<\|\nu_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty
; we set
w_{\delta}=\frac{\nu_{\delta}}{\|\nu_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}}
. Thus,
\|w_{\delta}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=1
and for fixed
u\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, by evenness of
\nu
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle T_{w_{\delta}}u(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w_{\delta}(y)u(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}w_{\delta}(y)u(x+y)\,\mathrm{d}y,\qquad\,.

Thus, by Jensen’s inequality

\displaystyle\|u-T_{w_{\delta}}u\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}[u(x)-u(x+h)]w_{\delta}(h)\,\mathrm{d}\,h\Big|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(x)-u(x+h)|^{p}w_{\delta}(h)\,\mathrm{d}\,h\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\|\nu_{\delta}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\iint\limits_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\mathbb{R}^{d}}|u(x)-u(x+h)|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}\,h\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq\|\nu_{\delta}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|u\|^{p}_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\,.

Accordingly, for a compact subset
K
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, taking into account the assumption (

I
2

) leads to

\displaystyle\|R_{K}-R_{K}T_{w_{\delta}}\|_{\mathcal{L}\big(W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,L^{p}(K)\big)}\leq\|\nu_{\delta}\|^{-1}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}0\,.

Thus the embedding
R_{K}:~W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to L^{p}(K)
is compact since by Lemma
3.80
, the operator
R_{K}T_{w_{\delta}}
is also compact for every
\delta>0
. In view of Theorem
3.79

\mathcal{K}\big(W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,L^{p}(K)\big)
is closed in
\mathcal{L}\big(W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\,L^{p}(K)\big)
. This prove the locally compactness of the embedding
W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Furthermore, it springs directly from the continuous embeddings,
W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
and the ideal property of compactor operators that the embedding
W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
is also compact.
∎

Remark 3.82
.

Note that (

I
′

1

) and (

I
2

) do not only capture the class of Lévy integrable functions which are non-integrable but also functions with strong singularity at the origin. For instance, consider
\nu(h)=|h|^{-d-\beta}
for
h\neq 0
with
\beta>0
. In this case, the
p
-Lévy integrability in (

I
1

) fails for
\beta\geq p
while (

I
′

1

) and (

I
2

) remain true and hence Theorem
3.81
applies.

In the sequel we will frequently use the family of cut-off functions introduced ion the following lemma.

Lemma 3.83
.

For
\delta>0
small enough, recall that
\Omega_{\delta}=\{x\in\Omega:\,\dist(x,\partial\Omega)>\delta\}
.
Let
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
supported in the unit ball
B_{1}(0)
,
\phi\geq 0
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=1
.
Then the function
\varphi_{\delta}(x)=\phi_{\delta/8}*\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{5\delta/8}}(x)
with
\phi_{\delta}(x)=\frac{1}{\delta^{d}}\phi(\frac{x}{\delta})
satisfies:
\varphi_{\delta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega),\,\supp\varphi_{\delta}\subset\Omega_{\delta/2},\,

0\leq\varphi_{\delta}\leq 1,\,\varphi_{\delta}=1\,\text{ on $\Omega_{\delta}$},\,

\varphi_{\delta}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}1
and
|\nabla\varphi_{\delta}|\leq c/\delta,(\mbox{with $c=8\int|\nabla\phi|$}).

Proof.
.

Indeed, since
\phi_{\delta/8}
is supported in
B_{\delta/8}(0)
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{\delta}(x)=\int\limits_{B_{\delta/8}(0)}\phi_{\delta/8}(y)\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{5\delta/8}}(x-y)\,\,\mathrm{d}y=\int\limits_{\Omega_{5\delta/8}}\phi_{\delta/8}(x-y)\,\,\mathrm{d}y.

Fix
x\in\Omega_{\delta}
. Let
z\in\partial\Omega
and
y\in B_{\delta/8}(0)
then
|x-z-y|\geq|x-z|-|y|\geq\frac{3\delta}{4}
. Since
z\in\partial\Omega
is arbitrarily chosen,
\dist(x-y,\partial\Omega)\geq\frac{3\delta}{4}>\frac{5\delta}{8}
we get
x-y\in\Omega_{5\delta/8}
i.e
\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{5\delta/8}}(x-y)=1
which implies

\varphi_{\delta}(x)=\int\limits_{B_{\delta/8}(0)}\phi_{\delta/8}(y)\,\,\mathrm{d}y=1.

That is
\varphi_{\delta}=1,
on
\Omega_{\delta}
. Hence we also get
\varphi_{\delta}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}1
. Now if
x\in\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}
then for
y\in\Omega_{5\delta/8}
we have

\displaystyle|x-y|\geq|\operatorname{dist}(y,\partial\Omega)-\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)|\geq\delta/8\,

so that
x-y\not\in B_{\delta/8}(0)
, i.e.
\phi_{\delta/8}(x-y)=0
for all
y\in\Omega_{5\delta/8}
which means that
\varphi_{\delta}(x)=0
that is
\operatorname{supp}\varphi_{\delta}\subset\Omega_{\delta/2}
. Last we note that
\nabla\varphi_{\delta}(x)=\frac{8}{\delta}[\nabla\phi]_{\delta/8}*\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{5\delta/8}}(x)
so that
|\nabla\varphi_{\delta}|\leq c/\delta
, (
with
c=8\int|\nabla\phi|
).

∎

As an immediate consequence of Theorem
3.81
we get the local compactness of
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
in
L^{p}(\Omega)
.

Corollary 3.84
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open bounded. Assume that
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathbb{R}
fulfills conditions (

I
1

) and (

I
2

). The embedding
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
is locally compact. Furthermore, for every bounded sequence
(u_{n})_{n}
there exits
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
and subsequence
(u_{n_{j}})_{j}
converging to
u
in
L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\Omega)
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\|u_{n}\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}.

Proof.
.

In view of Lemma
3.64
we claim that for
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\Omega)
, the mapping
J_{\varphi}:W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\to W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})
, with
J_{\varphi}u=u\varphi
is continuous and hence by Theorem
3.81
and the ideal property of compact operator, the linear map
J_{\varphi}:W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\to W_{\nu,\Omega}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\to L^{p}(\Omega)
is compact which therefore implies the locally compactness of the embedding
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
.

Let us prove the second statement.
For
\delta>0
small enough, we let
\varphi_{\delta}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
be such that
\varphi_{\delta}=1
on
\Omega_{\delta}
see in Lemma
3.83
.

Next we employ Cantor’s diagonalization procedure to show that every bounded sequence of
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
has a converging subsequence in
L_{\operatorname{loc}}^{2}(\Omega)
to some function
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
.
To this end, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
\delta_{k}=\frac{1}{2^{k+k_{0}}}
and merely set the shorthand notations
\Omega_{k}=\Omega_{\delta_{k}}
and
\varphi_{k}=\varphi_{\delta_{k}}
where
k_{0}
is large enough so that
\Omega_{\delta_{0}}
is non-empty. For every
k\geq 1
,
\overline{\Omega}_{k}\subset\Omega_{k+1}
and
\varphi_{k}=1
on
\overline{\Omega}_{k}
.

Assume
(u_{n})_{n}
is a bounded sequence in
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
. By the above remark, for each
k\geq 1
the sequence
(\varphi_{k}u_{n})_{n}
is also bounded in
W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and hence relatively compact in
L^{p}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by Theorem
3.81
. In particular for fixed
k,

(u_{n})_{n}
is relatively compact in
L^{p}(\Omega_{k})
because
\varphi_{k}u_{n}=u_{n}
on
\Omega_{k}
. Thus there is a subsequence
(u_{\theta_{k}(n)})_{n}
of
(u_{n})_{n}
converging to some function
u_{k}
in
L^{p}(\Omega_{k})
and almost everywhere in
\Omega_{k}
. Applying this argument again on
(\varphi_{k+1}u_{\theta_{k}(n)})_{n}
it turns out that the subsequence
(u_{\theta_{k}(n)})_{n}
possesses a further subsequence
(u_{\theta_{k+1}(n)})_{n}
converging to some function
u_{k+1}
in
L^{p}(\Omega_{k+1})
and a.e. on
\Omega_{k+1}
.

By this procedure, assume that for each
k
we have constructed a subsequence
(u_{\theta_{k}(n)})_{n}
having the property that the
(k+1)^{th}
subsequence
(u_{\theta_{k+1}(n)})_{n}
is a subsequence of the preceding
k^{th}
subsequence
(u_{\theta_{k}(n)})_{n}
and additionally the sequence
(u_{\theta_{k}(n)})_{n}
converges in
L^{p}(\Omega_{k})
and almost everywhere on
\Omega_{k}
to some function
u_{k}
.
Clearly, for each
k,
the restriction of
u_{k+1}
on
\Omega_{k}
coincides with
u_{k}
.
Therefore the function
u:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
coinciding with
u_{k}
on
\Omega_{k}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
is well defined and measurable since it is easy to verify that
u=u_{0}+\sum\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}u_{k}\mathds{1}_{\Omega_{k}\setminus\Omega_{k-1}}~~a.e
.
Now we consider the diagonal sequence
(u_{\theta_{n}(n)})_{n}
which is a bounded subsequence in
L^{p}(\Omega)
of the original sequence
(u_{n})_{n}
converging almost everywhere to
u
on
\Omega
. Indeed this follows immediately since
u_{\theta_{k}(n)}\to u_{k}~a.e
on
\Omega_{k}
and
(\Omega_{k})_{k}
is an exhaustion of
\Omega
. We conclude that
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
since
(u_{\theta_{n}(n)})_{n}
is bounded in
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
, and by Fatou’s lemma we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}\|u_{\theta_{n}(n)}\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}<\infty.

∎

Meanwhile, the global compactness requires some extra compatibility assumptions
between
\Omega
and
\nu
. We establish the global compactness, by exploiting the recent results from
[
JW19a
]
and
[
DMT18
]
. We intend to provide an alternative approach to the compactness result in
[
CDP18
, Theorem 2.2]
. The technique therein is adapted from
[
NPV12
, Theorem 7.1]
for fractional Sobolev spaces which uses the Sobolev extension property of the corresponding domain. However, the proof provided in
[
CDP18
]
seems to be valid only for domains which can be written as a finite union of cubes; unless
the corresponding nonlocal function space possesses the extension property. Our approach is rather standard and
follows the idea used to prove the classical Rellich–Kondrachov theorem, i.e. the compactness of the embedding
H^{1}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\Omega)
for
\Omega
sufficiently smooth. It consists of applying the local local compactness and using an approximation argument near the boundary of
\Omega
.

Let us introduce some regimes relating
\Omega
and
\nu
under which the global compactness holds true. We will enumerate these assumptions on the couple
(\nu,\Omega)
into different classes. We say that the couple
(\nu,\Omega)
is in the class
\mathscr{A}_{i},~~i=1,2,3
if
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is an open bounded set,
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
satisfies the conditions (

I
1

) and (

I
2

) and additionally
\nu
and
\Omega
satisfy:

\bullet
The class
\mathbf{\mathscr{A}_{1}}
: there exists an
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
-extension operator
E:W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\to W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. That is there is a constant
C:C(\nu,\Omega,d)>0
such that
\|u\|_{W^{p}_{\nu}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C\|u\|_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}
and
Eu|_{\Omega}=u
for every
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
.

\bullet
The class
\mathbf{\mathscr{A}_{2}}
:
\Omega
has Lipschitz boundary,
\nu
is radial and

(3.30)

\displaystyle q(\delta):=\frac{1}{\delta^{p}}\int_{B_{\delta}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}\infty.

\bullet
The class
\mathbf{\mathscr{A}_{3}}
: letting
\Omega_{\delta}=\{x\in\Omega:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)>\delta\}
for
\delta>0
, the following condition holds true

(3.31)

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}(\delta):=\inf_{a\in\partial\Omega}\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}\nu(h-a)\,\mathrm{d}h\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}\infty.

Let us also introduce a fourth class
\mathbf{\mathscr{A}_{4}}
of interest.

\bullet
The class
\mathbf{\mathscr{A}_{4}}:
we say that the couple
(\nu,\Omega)
is in the class
\mathbf{\mathscr{A}_{4}}
if
\Omega
is any open bounded subset of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
is a unimodal Lévy measure that is,
\nu
is radial, almost decreasing and
\nu\in L^{1}(\R^{d},1\land|h|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}h)
. Note that in the class
\mathscr{A}_{4},

\nu
is not necessarily singular near
0
.

Let us make some comments about the newly introduced classes. We note that the monotonicity of
\nu
is not required here. The condition (
3.31
) reflects a certain correlation between
\Omega
and the singularity of
\nu
near the origin when shifted on the boundary
\partial\Omega
of
\Omega
.
In a sense, the singularity of
\nu
is compatible with the boundary
\partial\Omega
. On the other hand,
\widetilde{q}(\delta)<\infty
since for each
a\in\partial\Omega
and each
\delta>0
,
\Omega_{\delta}\subset B^{c}_{\delta}(a)
and hence by (

I
1

) we get

\widetilde{q}(\delta)\leq\int_{B^{c}_{\delta}(0)}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h<\infty.

Straight away, we would like to show some examples of elements of the classes
\mathscr{A}_{i}
,
i=1,2,3
. To this end, let us recall some concepts about the regularity of a domain.

Recall that
\Omega
is of class
C^{1,1}
if for every
a\in\partial\Omega
there is
r>0
for which
B_{r}(a)\cap\partial\Omega=\{x=(x^{\prime},x_{d})\in B_{r}(a)~:x_{d}=\gamma(x^{\prime})\}
represents the graph of a
C^{1,1}
function
\gamma:\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\to\mathbb{R}
. That is to say
\gamma
is a
C^{1}
function whose gradient is Lipschitz.
The main result in
[
Bar09
]
shows that an open set
\Omega
is
C^{1,1}
if and only if
\Omega
satisfies the interior and exterior sphere condition. We say that
\Omega
satisfies the interior and exterior sphere condition at some scale
r>0
if for every
a\in\partial\Omega
one can find
a^{\prime}\in\Omega
and
a^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\Omega}^{c}
for which
B_{r}(a^{\prime})\subset\Omega
,
B_{r}(a^{\prime\prime})\subset\overline{\Omega}^{c}
and
\overline{B_{r}(a^{\prime})}\cap\overline{B_{r}(a^{\prime\prime})}=\{a\}
.
The interior and exterior sphere condition holds for every scale
r\in(0,r_{0})
once it holds for
r_{0}
. This characterization entails that a
C^{1,1}
set
\Omega
is a
d
-set (or
volume density condition
according to some authors): that is, there exist two positive constants
c>0
and
r_{0}>0
such that for every
r\in(0,r_{0})
and every
a\in\partial\Omega

\displaystyle|\Omega\cap B_{r}(a)|\geq cr^{d}.

Now we show that the classes
\mathscr{A}_{i},~i=1,2,3
are not empty. Let us simply assume that (

I
′

2

) holds true, take for instance
\nu(h)=|h|^{-d-sp},
(
s\in(0,1)
), which together with (

I
1

) implies (

I
2

). It is easy to see that

q(\delta)=\frac{1}{\delta^{p}}\int_{B_{\delta}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h=\frac{|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|}{p(1-s)}\delta^{-sp}\xrightarrow{\delta\to 0}\infty.

This shows that (
3.30
) is verified. Wherefore, considering any Lipschitz domain
\Omega
,
(\nu,\Omega)
in the class
\mathscr{A}_{2}
.

If
\Omega
is of class
C^{1,1}
we would like to show that
(\nu,\Omega)
is in the class
\mathscr{A}_{3}
. Consider
R>0
,
\delta_{0}
and
r_{0}>0
as above. Fix
a\in\partial\Omega
, by the interior sphere condition, consider
\delta\in(0,\delta_{0}/4)
small enough and
x\in\Omega
such that
B_{2\delta}(x)\subset\Omega
,
\operatorname{dsit}(x,\partial\Omega)=|x-a|=2\delta
and
\overline{B_{2\delta}(x)}\cap\partial\Omega=\{a\}
then obviously,
B_{\delta}(x)\subset\Omega_{\delta}\cap B_{2\delta}(x)\subset\Omega_{\delta}\cap B_{4\delta}(a)
. This yields that

(3.32)

\displaystyle|\Omega_{\delta}\cap B_{4\delta}(a)|\geq d\omega_{d}\delta^{d},\quad\text{with}~~\omega_{d}=|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|.

Therefore, recalling that
\nu(h-a)\geq M|h-a|^{-d}\geq\frac{M}{4^{d}\delta^{d}}
when
h\in B_{4\delta}(a)
we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}\nu(h-a)\,\mathrm{d}h\geq\frac{R}{4^{d}\delta^{d}}\int_{\Omega_{\delta}\cap B_{4\delta}(a)}\hskip-12.91663pt\,\mathrm{d}h=\,\frac{R}{4^{d}\delta^{d}}|\Omega_{\delta}\cap B_{4\delta}(a)|\geq\frac{d\omega_{d}}{4^{d}}R.

Finally, we get
\widetilde{q}(\delta)\geq\frac{d\omega_{d}}{4^{d}}R
which means that the condition (
3.31
) is verified since
M
can be arbitrarily large. Thus if
\Omega
is
C^{1,1}
and
\nu
satisfying (

I
′

2

),
(\nu,\Omega)
belongs to
\mathscr{A}_{3}
.

On the other hand, it is well known from
[
Zho15
]
that
\Omega
is an extension domain for
W^{s,p}(\Omega),~s\in(0,1)
if and only if
\Omega
is a
d
-set and thus,
(|\cdot|^{-d-sp},\Omega)
is an element of the class
\mathscr{A}_{1}
.

Assuming (

I
1

) and (

I
2

) it is an interesting question to know under which additional minimal conditions on boundary
\partial\Omega
and
\nu
the condition (
3.31
) holds true. We illustrate this interest, with a simple counter example.
In the Euclidean plane consider
\nu(h)=|h|^{-2-\alpha}\mathds{1}_{V}(h)\,\,(d=2,p=2,\alpha\in(0,2))
with
V=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:~|x_{1}|<|x_{2}|\}
and
\Omega=\{(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:~4|x_{2}-6|<x_{1},~0<x_{1}<4\}
whose boundary is continuous. Considering
a=(0,6)\in\partial\Omega
one has
V\cap(\Omega_{\delta}-a)=\emptyset
for every
\delta>0
(see figure
2
). Therefore we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{q}(\delta)\leq\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}\nu(h-a)\,\mathrm{d}h=0.

In conclusion, the condition (
3.31
) fails although conditions (

I
1

) and (

I
2

) are satisfied.

Figure 2:

It is interesting to know whether for small
\delta>0
,
\Omega_{\delta}
inherits the regularity of
\Omega
. As proven in
[
GT15
, Section 6.14]
if
\Omega
is of class
C^{k}
with
k\geq 2
then so is
\Omega_{\delta}
. Regarding the relevance of the relation (
3.32
), we introduce the notion of
strong volume density condition
which, together with (

I
′

2

) will imply (
3.31
).

Definition 3.85
.

We say that
\Omega
satisfies
the strong volume density condition
if there exist positive constants
r_{0}>0
and
c>0
such that for all
\delta,r\in(0,r_{0})
and

a\in\partial\Omega
, one has
|\Omega_{\delta}\cap B_{r}(a)|\geq cr^{d}.

Let us resume with our quest toward the global compactness. In the spirit of
[
DMT18
]
and
[
Pon03
]
we will need some estimates near the boundary
\partial\Omega
. We start with an inequality involving cut-off functions.

Lemma 3.86
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open and bounded . Assume
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
be an even measurable function. For
\delta>0
small enough, let
\varphi\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
be such that
\varphi=0
on
\Omega_{\delta}
,
\varphi=1
on
\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}
,
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
and
|\nabla\varphi|\leq c/\delta
(cf. Lemma
3.83
by taking
\varphi=1-\varphi_{\delta}
). Then for every
u\in L^{p}(\Omega)
, the following estimate holds true

(3.33)

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}\big|[u\varphi](x)-[u\varphi](y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{p}}\int\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x+2^{p}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y

where,
C=2^{p}c^{p}\int\limits_{B_{R}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h
and
R=\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)
.

Proof.
.

Firstly, since
\varphi=1
on
\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}
we have

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}~\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}\hskip-17.22217pt\big|[u\varphi](x)-[u\varphi](y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\hskip-12.91663pt\iint\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}~\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}\hskip-17.22217pt|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\,.

In view of the fact that
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
and
|\varphi(x)-\varphi(y)|\leq c/\delta|x-y|
for every
x,y\in\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle\big|[u\varphi](x)-[u\varphi](y)\big|^{p}

\displaystyle=\big(\varphi(y)(u(x)-u(y))+u(x)(\varphi(x)-\varphi(y))\big)^{p}

(3.34)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}+\frac{2^{p-1}c^{p}}{\delta^{p}}|u(x)|^{p}|x-y|^{p}\,.

Secondly, noticing that
\Omega\subset B_{R}(x)
for all
x\in\Omega
where
R=\operatorname{diam}(\Omega)
and integrating both sides of (
3.34
) over
\Omega_{\delta/2}\times\Omega_{\delta/2}
we obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}\Omega_{\delta/2}}\big|[u\varphi](x)-[u\varphi](y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y+\frac{2^{p-1}c^{p}}{\delta^{p}}\int\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\int\limits_{B_{R}(x)}|x-y|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=2^{p-1}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y+\frac{2^{p-1}c^{p}}{\delta^{p}}\Big(\int\limits_{B_{R}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\Big)\int\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Similar to the previous estimate, using (
3.34
) we get

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}\times\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}\big|[u\varphi](x)-[u\varphi](y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p-1}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y+\frac{2^{p-1}c^{p}}{\delta^{p}}\Big(\int\limits_{B_{R}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\Big)\int\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

Altogether, the desired estimate follows as claimed since by symmetry we can use the split

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega\times\Omega}=\iint\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}\times\Omega_{\delta/2}}+\,2\hskip-8.61108pt\iint\limits_{\Omega_{\delta/2}\times\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}+\iint\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}\times\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}\,.

∎

Since Lipschitz domains play an important role in this section let us recall another handy characterization of such domains according to
[
Gri11
, Theorem 1.2.2.2]
.

Theorem 3.87
.

A bounded open subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
has the uniform cone properties if and only if its boundary is Lipschitz. In particular any open bounded convex set is Lipschitz.

Let us recall the following:
\Omega
has the uniform segment property (resp. cone property ) if for every
x\in\partial\Omega
, there exists a neighborhood of
x
in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and new coordinate system
\{y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{d}\}
such that

1.

V is a hypercube,
V=\{(y_{1},y_{2},\cdots,y_{d})~:-a_{i}\leq y_{i}\leq a_{i}~~i=1,2,\cdots,d\}
.

2.

y-z\in\Omega
whenever
y\in\overline{\Omega}\cap V
and
z\in C
where
C
is the open segment
\{(0,0,\cdots,z_{d})~:0<z_{d}<h\}
(resp. the open cone
\{(z^{\prime},z_{d})~:\cot\theta|z^{\prime}|<z_{d}<h\}
for some
\theta\in(0,\pi/2]
) for some
h>0
.

Note that
\tan\theta<1/k
where is
k
the uniform Lipschitz constant or
\Omega
.

We need the following lemma in the sequel.

Lemma 3.88
.

Assume
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is open bounded and
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
is an even function. Then for every
u\in L^{p}(\Omega)
and every
\delta>0
small enough there exists a positive constant
C>0
independent of
\delta
such that

(3.35)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{p}\widetilde{q}(2\delta)}\int_{\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2^{p+1}}{\widetilde{q}(2\delta)}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y.

Moreover if
\Omega
has Lipschitz boundary and
\nu
is radial then

(3.36)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\frac{C}{\delta^{p}q(2\delta)}\int_{\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x+\frac{2^{p+1}}{q(2\delta)}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y.

Proof.
.

Let
\varphi
be as in Lemma
3.86
and fix
a\in\partial\Omega
. A routine check reveals that
\Omega_{2\delta}-a\subset\Omega_{\delta}-x
for every
x\in\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a)
which yields,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a)}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a)}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\Omega_{\delta}-x}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h

\displaystyle\geq\int_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a)}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\Omega_{2\delta}-a}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{q}(2\delta)\int_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a)}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x.

By a compactness argument there exist
a^{1},a^{2},\cdots a^{n}\in\partial\Omega
such that
\partial\Omega\subset\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}B_{\delta/2}(a^{i})
. Thus,
\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}\subset\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a^{i})\subset\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}
.
Since
\varphi u=0
on
\Omega_{\delta/2}
so on
\Omega_{\delta}
. Therefore with the aid of the above estimate we get the following estimate

\displaystyle\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}\big|[\varphi u](x)-[\varphi u](y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y\geq 2\iint\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta}~\Omega_{\delta}}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\geq\int\limits_{\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a^{i})}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\int_{\Omega_{\delta}}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}y\geq 2\widetilde{q}(2\delta)\int\limits_{\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a^{i})}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq 2\widetilde{q}(2\delta)\int\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x=2\widetilde{q}(2\delta)\int\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}|u(x)|^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\,.

This combined with (
3.33
) gives (
3.35
).

Now we assume that
\nu
is radial and
\Omega
is Lipschitz. Let
a\in\partial\Omega
up to translating we may
a=0
. Since
[u\varphi]=0
on
\Omega_{\delta}
, according to
[
Pon03
, Eq. 22]
there is a constant
C=C(\Omega,d,p)
not depending on
u\varphi
such that for all
w\in\Lambda\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}

(3.37)

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta/2}(0)}|[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\delta^{p}\int_{\Omega\cap B_{3\delta}(0)}\frac{|[u\varphi](x+rw)-[u\varphi]((x)|^{p}}{r^{p}}\,\mathrm{d}x

Here
\Lambda
is a half cone locally related to
\Omega
and defined by
\Lambda=\{x=(x^{\prime},x_{d}):\,|x^{\prime}|\leq x_{d}\}
.

In connection to the polar coordinates, since
\nu
is radial, integrating the above inequality with respect to the measure
|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h:=r^{p+d-1}\nu(r)\,\mathrm{d}\sigma_{d-1}(w)\,\mathrm{d}r
over
\Lambda\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\times(0,\delta)
and letting
c_{1}=|\Lambda\cap\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|/|\mathbb{S}^{d-1}|
gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big(c_{1}\int\limits_{B_{\delta}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\Big)\int\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta/2}(0)}|[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x&\leq C\delta^{p}\iint\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{3\delta}(0)\,B_{\delta}(0)}|[u\varphi](x+h)-[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\,\mathrm{d}x\\
&\leq C\delta^{p}\iint\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{4\delta}(0)\,\Omega\cap B_{4\delta}(0)}|[u\varphi](y)-[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\nu(y-x)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x,\end{split}

where the last inequality holds since
x+h\in\Omega\cap B_{4\delta}(0)
for
x\in\Omega\cap B_{3\delta}(0)
and
h\in B_{\delta}(0)
. Up to doubling the parameter
\delta>0
relabelling the constant
C>0
that is

\displaystyle\Big(\int\limits_{B_{2\delta}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\Big)\int\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(0)}|[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq C\delta^{p}\iint\limits_{\Omega\cap B_{8\delta}(0)\,\Omega\cap B_{8\delta}(0)}|[u\varphi](y)-[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\nu(y-x)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x

and using once more a recovering argument as previously (since
\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}\subset\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}\Omega\cap B_{\delta}(a^{i})\subset\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{n}\Omega\cap B_{8\delta}(a^{i})
) one reaches the following estimate

(3.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Big(\int\limits_{B_{2\delta}(0)}|h|^{p}\nu(h)\,\mathrm{d}h\Big)\int\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}|[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x&\leq C\delta^{p}\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}|[u\varphi](y)-[u\varphi](x)|^{p}\nu(y-x)\,\mathrm{d}y\,\mathrm{d}x.\end{split}

Hence,

\displaystyle 2q(2\delta)\int\limits_{\Omega\setminus\Omega_{\delta/2}}[\varphi u]^{p}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\leq C\iint\limits_{\Omega\Omega}\big|[\varphi u](x)-[\varphi u](y)\big|^{p}\nu(x-y)\,\mathrm{d}x\,\mathrm{d}y.

which combined with (
3.33
) implies (
3.36
).

∎

Here is the global compactness result.

Theorem 3.89
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be open, bounded and
\nu:\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}\to[0,\infty]
be a measurable function. If the couple
(\nu,\Omega)
belongs to one of the class
\mathscr{A}_{i},~i=1,2,3
then the embedding
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
is compact. In particular, the embedding
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
is compact.

Proof.
.

Given the continuous embedding
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega|\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
, it will be sufficient to prove that the embedding
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)\hookrightarrow L^{p}(\Omega)
is compact. For
(\nu,\Omega)
belonging to the class
\mathscr{A}_{1}
the result is a direct consequence of Theorem
3.81
. Now assume
(\nu,\Omega)
belongs to o the class
\mathscr{A}_{2}
(resp.
\mathscr{A}_{3}
) then for
\varepsilon>0
there is
\delta>0
small enough such that
2^{p+1}q^{-1}(2\delta)<\varepsilon
(resp.
2^{p+1}\widetilde{q}^{-1}(2\delta)<\varepsilon
) If
(u_{n})_{n}
is a bounded sequence of
W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
then Corollary
3.84
infers the existence of a subsequence
(u_{n_{j}})_{j}
of
(u_{n})_{n}
converging to some
u\in W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)
in
L^{p}(\Omega_{\delta/2})
i.e
\|u_{n_{j}}-u\|_{L^{p}(\Omega_{\delta/2})}\to 0
as
j\to\infty
. In any case, in view of Lemma
3.88
, passing to the limsup in (
3.35
) or in (
3.36
) applied to
u_{n_{j}}-u
we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{j\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}|u_{n_{j}}(x)-u(x)|^{p}\,\mathrm{d}x\leq M\varepsilon

where
M=2^{p}\|u\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}+2^{p}\sup\limits_{n\geq 1}\|u_{n}\|^{p}_{W_{\nu}^{p}(\Omega)}<\infty.
Finally, letting
\varepsilon\to 0
gives
\limsup\limits_{j\to\infty}\|u_{n_{j}}-u\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}=0
. Which ends the proof.
∎

A noteworthy consequence of Theorem
3.89
is the well known and established Rellich-Kondrachov’s
11
11

11

The classical Rellich-Kondrachov theorem is often known as the Kondrachov compactness theorem after V.Kondrachov who generalized Rellich’s result for compact map
W^{1,p}_{0}(\Omega)
into
L^{q}(\Omega)
whenever
1\leq q\leq dp/(d-p)
.
.

Corollary 3.90

(Rellich-Kondrachov’s Theorem)
.

Assume
